ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e1691	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) It is true that such a link is provided for in the draft document 'Agenda 2000'. Austria also rejects the idea of being able to define such conditions nationally, because this would result in different environmental standards of the individual countries; this difference would also be rejected by us. If the idea arose that such a link, which we reject in itself, would call into question participation in the environmental programme, it would also be strongly rejected by Austria.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e2954	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e2970	Deputies! We can be proud that the Austrian farmers have practically adopted the environmental programme from 1995 onwards. We are the only country in which global work is being carried out on the ecologicalisation of agriculture. I would like to give you a few examples: In the field of plant protection products, the active substance use has decreased by around 920 tonnes or around 20 percent. The use of organisms, by natural pest fighters, has been greatly increased. It is true that we have an increase in the area on which these products, these beneficiaries, are used, of 35 percent. Austria also has a restrictive path in the field of plant protection products and recognition of plant protection products. While 850 active substances are authorised in the EU, only about 250 active substances are authorised in Austria. In the field of mineral fertilizers, a 7 per cent reduction is observed in the calendar year 1996 compared to the previous period, and in comparison to the peaks from the mid-1970s, Austrian fertilizer use has declined by almost half. This is a balance sheet that can be seen.

According to § 37 para. 1 of the phytosanitary legislation 1997 is due to expire on 1 January 1997. For the purposes of this Act, plant protection products authorised in the phytosanitary register as registered plant protection products are authorised on August 1997, in the phytosanitary register. The authorisation of these plant protection products shall end at the latest with 26. ==References== – This was mentioned in the letter you addressed. My question: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00176/index.shtml -- 176/M]] What key agricultural policy objectives do you want to achieve in the European Union under Agenda 2000 which deals with the EU financial framework, the development of the common agricultural policy, structural policy and the enlargement of the East?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e4529	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e4545	Deputies! I will try to address the key points in the short time available to me. First: The agenda will not be approved by Austria in the form currently in place. Secondly, Where is she to improve? . We reject price reductions, for example in the area of cereals, and we are committed to strengthening the production of energy and raw materials as a possibility. We reject the regulation in the field of oilseeds and silomania. In the milk sector, we are in favour of safeguarding the quota and introducing a cow premium. We have no understanding – and we do not see the need for this – of price reductions if they do not result from market developments. In the cattle sector, we are also not satisfied with the price reduction. We have to put in place measures to ensure farmers' incomes, for example in the field of premiums, but on the other hand to have a positive impact on market development, such as promotion measures. In the field of rural structural policy, we agree that, in order to support the initiatives that are intended to apply to the whole rural area, the whole rural area is regarded as a support scene. We are not satisfied with the national differentiations in eco-pulptures or with modulation, as I have already mentioned. With regard to the financial perspective, we believe that it is necessary that the guideline for budgeting in the European Union is still 1.27% of gross social product and that the agricultural guideline remains unchanged. With regard to the enlargement of the East, I am in favour of ensuring that measures such as transitional arrangements for all four freedoms which allow for the enlargement of Austria but also for the people of those countries are safeguarded. I am thinking, for example, of the need to regulate agriculture in particular, that products should not be unloaded on the first commercial market, in this case possibly in Austria, but rather distributed justly and evenly across the European Union, or on measures on this side of the border, i.e. in the Austrian field, in order to be able to provide transitional aid if necessary. Austria is entering the agenda for additional measures; I have already mentioned the amount of the base. I am also thinking of the model of harvest insurance that we want to anchor during the agenda discussion in Europe. So we have a clear concept and are currently looking for partners for its implementation. We'll find these partners.

It is precisely the opposition – I understand that – that in recent days compromises have been made, compromises on the way to negotiation which are under way today. Of course, we have accepted compromises in those areas that have been concerned with safeguarding trust, which have dealt with transitional provisions relating to hardness clauses; Time limits and cover solutions have been introduced. But the principles of this pension reform, which we have undertaken, have not made any commotions: Yes to the protection of trust, yes to the suspension of hardships, but also a clear yes to maintain the important principles of this pension reform. In the end, this pension reform brings changes which have not yet been made to this form, but which are necessary in order to ensure future pensions. These changes lead us to a way that is clearly defined. The path is clearly defined, the goal is clear, we will continue along this path – at a speed that we have now defined. Some people could have imagined a slightly higher speed in some areas, including the question of the calculation period in the ASVG area, but the harmonisation with the public service is also, for me, the higher good. We will, as is so common practice, occasionally maintain and check where we stand, how the framework conditions have changed, what is to be corrected. But I think the path to be taken is clear, lies ahead of us and is clearly defined by this pension reform. [[President Dr. Brauneder shall take over the chairmanship.]] I am also absolutely convinced that this path is not only clear for the next few years, but that we do not need a stopover, not a stop over the next few years, but that this pension reform is good for the years up to the next millennium. Mrs Hostasch has already said that, from the point of view of the ASVG negotiators and the ASVG managers, the harmonisation that has been achieved with this pension reform is above all what has been achieved otherwise. Harmonization of pension systems, particularly with that of public services, the task of what has repeatedly led to a mutual blockade over the last few years and made it possible to reform political reality, which is the most important thing. It is a long-term step until 2020, but then we have the 18-year calculation period in all systems. This harmonisation is at the forefront – with some others. Ladies and gentlemen! However, important structural reform has also been achieved in many other areas. If you had told me half a year ago that we would succeed in bringing all income from work into social security, in bringing about harmonisation steps in the area of subsidiarity, in any event in bringing about a reasonable solution to the inclusion of minor employees from today's point of view, I would not have believed it. I would not have believed that we can say today - without a long discussion on this - that this pension reform in reality has also solved the work contract issue and has been able to bring about the greatest uncertainties and misconceptions of clarification. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] However, this pension reform is also a clear signal to and for older workers – and I am completely in agreement with Mrs Hostasch and probably most of the ladies and gentlemen here in the House – that we have to end slowly with an Austrian maladministration, which motivates people to retire all too early for various reasons. I know that this started in a certain area of industry years ago, and that has continued, and unfortunately it has become so to speak saloonable to God and has been a sort of good thing to send older people into retirement, on the one hand, and on the other hand to make the wish come to older people, as soon as possible when it goes into retirement. It's not supposed to be like that! However, this should and cannot be the case only if, at the same time, we are also putting in place accompanying measures that enable longer employment of older people to motivate older people to stay in employment longer. I do not want to mention the individual points – already referred to by Mrs Hostasch and known to you – but the political objective must be clear to us: It should be of interest to all parties involved – employers, employees and only the pension insurers – to ensure that people in this country remain in employment again. I do not have to mention the increased life expectancy here, nor do I have to say that people of higher age today are more healthy than they were 10, 20 or 30 years ago and that of course we also want to support this development. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The third message goes to the boys. This pension reform is an important step towards securing the generation contract. It is a response to that we know that, if we were to maintain the status quo, we would have come to a federal contribution of 2.5 percent to the pensions from today to the year 2020 – plus 2.5 percent of our gross domestic product. Let us remember how difficult it is to present 2.5% of our gross domestic product as net new debt within the framework of the Maastricht criteria and not exceed that. The maintenance of the status quo alone would have brought us this plus 2.5 percent by 2020. But it is also quite right – and there really is: repetitio est mater studiorum; this cannot be repeated often enough – that this pension reform does not bring a reduction of pensions. No, no, no, No, it merely brings in the rise of our pensions, which we expect over the next 10, 15 and 20 years. We have to tell people that too! Politics is about to be afraid, not to be afraid. Dear Dr. Hazel-steiner, this also applies to opposition! [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] Finally, I am pleased that in two, as I believe, very essential areas have succeeded in implementing suspension measures with this pension reform. This does not happen by chance to women, but, of course, it was the government's intention to help women do family work, that is, unpaid family work. On the one hand, there is a better consideration of child rearing times. From my point of view, it is also clearly underestimated what has been done here. The increase in the basis of assessment by the year 2000 to the then likely compensatory allowance rate of around 8 100 S means an increase in their pension by 2 400 S per woman and child per year. This is a plus of 35 percent from the title "Children's Education". Finally, I am also very grateful to Mrs. Hostasch for having started to discuss from his own point of view – and we have agreed very quickly – such women – it must, I believe, not necessarily be women, but it is mostly women – who are caring for family members and thus get out of a job, to facilitate further insurance for the pension by paying employers' contributions to pension insurance insurance. This is a second important point. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and SPÖ.]] I can conclude by saying: I hope that our pension system of the future will be based on three pillars – not only on those you propose, honourable Member Gaugg; I do not want to see this in our pension system or our pensioners. We already want some sort of security. I think that the change from a payment procedure to a capital cover procedure within a pillar is either not feasible or at least a path that is adventurous. I'll tell you that very clearly. Of course, public pension schemes will also have to be based on the payment procedure in the future. Of course, for all that is done in the private sector, the capital coverage process is the thing that is given. And this path is going on, too. We will have to take care of the second and third pillars. The first steps, as Mr Ruttenstorfer told me, will soon become politically familiar. And the major step towards a fiscal advantage of creating an occupational pension scheme is a very important element in the fiscal reform of the year 2000. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! This pension reform, which has been successful here, is a tangible package. It is true that in the meantime the storks have flown away from rest, but they just don't stay in Austria for so long. Sometimes I had the impression that some would have leaned back a little and waited so long at the beginning of the negotiations until these storks had flown away. But we have come to a good end here. We have significantly accelerated the pace of the negotiations when it went towards the finale, as Mr Nürnberger has said. This pension reform is a good piece of work. And I am very pleased that it was not only possible in the consensus of the two coalition partners – that is, of course – but also in agreement with the social partners and that it was possible to complete this large amount of reform work for Austria and for our citizens.

[[Oh! and Phui! call at the SPÖ]] [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] I should like to point out once again that the Austrian National Council decided before the summer a radical re-regulation of all political pensions. This regulation was not only made by politicians themselves, but, in order to rule out these doubts, an independent committee of experts was set up which had to draw up a proposal. I should like to remind you that this committee of experts was chaired by the President of the Court of Auditors, Dr Franz Fierce. Members of the Chamber of Economic Trustees, the Bar Association and many other experts belonged to the Commission. The proposal put forward by this Commission has been implemented in a radical manner and has been adopted in the framework of the re-regulation of four parties to this House. The four parties agreed on a referee reform in 1997 with the following points: 1. Political relations are disconnected from the official's schedule. 2. Precipices are removed. 3rd. Only a uniform reference is required. 4. Adjustment of remuneration due to general wage developments. 5. Income pyramid. 6. Abolition of political pensions – politicians remain in their respective pension system. 7. Absence of the discharges. 8. Detailed income report on economic and political income. 9. The abolition of unemployment income in the Constitutional Court. 10. 7 000 S monthly payment to the service provider for the private use of the service car. 11th@item: inlistbox Incompatibilities for Members of Parliament. 12. Publication of a lobbyist list. 13. Upper limits for National Bank, Social Security and Chambers. 14. Limits for public cash income. 15. Limits for land and municipal contributions. 16. Reimbursement of travel costs by a remote zone model. They see that this is a comprehensive reform that includes all the points and that there will be a conversion period for politicians in the future. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to take this opportunity to remind you of the amount of your pension contributions or pension contributions. [[the speaker turns toward the freedom]] In the National Council, in the Federal Council and in the EU Parliament, the pension contributions for the Freedom Party amount to 1. January 1995 at 18.49 percent, the pension contributions range from 1.5 to 5.61 percent. For the sake of good order, I would also like to mention the contributions for top-level organs. Pension contributions are for members of the government of 1. January 1995 to 21.49 percent, the respective pension contributions range from 1.5 to 5.61 percent. In this context, I would also like to quote the opinion of the Constitutional Service of 18 June. February 1997, which reads as follows: In view of its broad reform proposals, the Commission has therefore also recommended, in particular of the right to pension, that transitional arrangements be provided at least for those active politicians who have already acquired expectations under applicable law and have also partly paid insignificant contributions. In this context, it seemed possible for the Commission to make all proposed changes apply only to those who first entered politics, while the right to apply to the already active politicians would continue to apply. It was noted that successive transposition to the new legal situation would result in a relatively long period of time. From a constitutional point of view, the Commission's proposal is to say that this procedure would be entirely in line with the constitutional framework and would not be necessary to do so. High house! The claim made on several occasions in the ground that ASVG insured persons will receive massive pension reductions by the renewal of the Labour and Social Rights Amendment Act 1997 is not applicable because the reduction in net replacement rates will only be very balanced and socially balanced and will be effective in longer periods and will not be expected to reduce future pensions due to income development, but will remain equal to the current real level of pension. The possibilities of sliding pensions were extended in the ASVG. You have already had an opportunity to discuss this regulation and many other important new regulations in this House. This discussion will continue after the present urgent request. High house! It should be noted that the Boards and social security bodies are fully covered by the ASVG regulations as regards pension insurance law. It is true, however, that in these areas additional schemes exist in the form of occupational pensions, as provided for in your three-pillar model. Occupational pension schemes exist not only in chambers and social security institutions, but also in numerous other companies. For the grant pension, employees of the Chambers of Workers must also pay a pension contribution according to the ASVG in addition to the pension contribution. For the sake of completeness, I would point out the level of contributions. They are higher than the maximum contribution base of 7.25 percent or from the double maximum contribution base to 8 percent and will increase to up to 10 percent over the next two years. It should also be noted that in recent years the occupational pension schemes in chambers and social security institutions have undergone fundamental changes in the direction of reductions in benefits for future pensioners. These changes cannot be made in a constitutional manner by law, but only by means of appropriate collective legal standard or other legal standard, mostly in the individual contract. Like other banks, the Austrian National Bank has its own pension system. Reforms in recent years, as I have been informed, have already made substantial savings at the pension fund of the Austrian National Bank, especially by introducing two per cent pension contributions without maximum calculation in old contracts or ten per cent pension contributions in new contracts since April 1993. The retirement age was set at 60 years of age or 40 years of service. With reference to resolution No. E 58 of 15. In May 1997, the upcoming National Bank Act will ensure that new staff will receive only an ASVG-pension supplemented by a pension fund. In the case of the regulations in the Austrian National Bank, it should be noted that the service and pension conditions are based on the standards of other banks, in particular in view of the fact that a well-trained staff should be available to meet the requirements of the future economic and monetary union, and also for the purpose of being able to carry out the tasks in both national and international monetary policy in the interests of Austria. High house! Today's decision on pension reform is a principle decision in the best sense of the word. It certainly contributes to greater justice and harmonisation. The reform on the agenda today will result in those who have more to contribute to these reforms than those who have fewer. The Federal Government has made itself concerned with the social balance, the harmonisation and a good piece of future security of young people and therefore implemented this pension reform. These objectives will be achieved by the decision of Wednesday and, in continuation, today's decision. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e16591	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! I would like to highlight the issue of "pension reform" from the point of view of an elderly person or from the point of view of the older generation. As a people's representative, we should not forget that in addition to all the cost factors and calculations, mathematical formulas, this problem has a profound human dimension. It's about fates, it's about people, it's about the older generation. And the difference between the disciples and the older ones is that the older people no longer have the opportunity to change essential factors of their lives. And so I would like to say that we should actually be glad that people get older, that they stay healthy longer, that they have the chance to live a longer life. The highest good that we humans possess is life. And that generation that is about to retire or have already retired has been the first to pay full pension contributions for a lifetime. The generations before have very probably taken advantage of the payment process of benefits of the disciples. But now that generation comes into retirement, which has paid a lifetime. People see the pension system as a socially organised form of saving. There must be three principles in this general form of saving, and I would like to mention them: The first is the so-called trust principle. In other words, every elder must know that existing pensions are not affected because, as I have already mentioned, he has no way of changing the factors that are relevant to them. If one highlights the situation in Austria that only about 7.5 percent of private insurance is available and only about 9 percent can have an occupational pension benefit, then it is recognized that much more than 90 percent depend on the state pension as on the daily bread. And that makes their meaning so striking to our people. The second condition is the principle of security of value. I am pleased to have been able to participate personally in the negotiations which set out the ASVG sanctions for 1998. Despite the strained budgetary situation, it has succeeded in ensuring an overall increase of 1.5 percent. During the same period, the inflation rate is 1.2 percent. That is, there is a nominal increase, but also a real increase. What is particularly gratifying is that there is a fixed amount of 430 million schilling in this budget for the compensation recipients, which can really be awarded according to social criteria. Overall, the increase is a sum of 3.9 billion schilling. This is the enormous effort that this government, but also the Austrian taxpayer who has to make available this money, is making in order to ensure value assurance for the elderly. The third condition that is necessary is a guarantee of the future. It can be seen that Austria is not alone with the pressure to implement a pension reform. I have looked closely at the data from Germany today. Germany has the option of either raising the pension contributions by 49 percent or reducing benefits by 42 percent or letting them work longer by 10.6 years per capita. Today I have taken the media that Germany chooses the way to raise the contributions to 21 percent. So huge benefits are required of the population. Austria is taking a good middle path here. This pension reform is a compromise – that is what I say to the opposition address. Mr President, today we are talking about a procedure by hand in the mouth. Mr President, I should like to thank you I agree with the question... [[Abg. Meisinger: No, he quoted!]] He quoted it; yes, he found it here. He has identified himself with this statement. I allow myself to ask the question: What chance did Austria have in 1945? Where were the reserves and the funds from the past seven years, i.e. from 1938 to 1945, with which one could have paid pensions in 1945? – These were not there, and Austria had no other chance at all. We are therefore in favour of this form of financing, and our work and the diligence of the Austrian population are the guarantee that pensions can continue to be paid in the future.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e17301	Mr President. Mrs. Federal Minister! The aim of the pension concept is to preserve our quality and advanced pension system and to secure it for future generations. Socially acceptable changes within the system should increase acceptance and facilitate financial viability in the future. In addition to its original purpose, namely as an income replacement in old age and in the event of disability or in the event of incapacity for work, a number of other purposes have been transferred to the pension system over time, all of which are to be fulfilled at the same time. This pension concept has three main objectives: the approximation of the systems, the strengthening of the generation contract and also greater transparency of the objectives. One important point is also the abolition of subsidiarity in health insurance. The exception to the fact that the GSVG does not require health insurance insurers to make a health insurance contribution if, for example, they are already subject to compulsory insurance in the ASVG is repealed and replaced by a supplementary health insurance insurance scheme. Ladies and gentlemen! So far, it has also been a valid right that farmers should not have to pay health insurance if they have been insured with a close family. This scheme is now to expire. As far as possible, transitional provisions shall ensure that: Gently effective withdrawal. The whole package of measures, whether it be the changes in labour law, changes in labour market policy and also changes to the Social Security Act, aims to increase justice between the different professional groups and between younger and older generations. In addition, ladies and gentlemen, the aim is to ensure efficiency, acceptance, transparency and long-term financial viability.

I am very glad that the principle of free will between workers and employers has been largely enshrined and that we have distanced ourselves from the originally intended forced arrangements. Fifthly, I would like to comment on that question, ladies and gentlemen, which has been discussed over and over again in the last few days: Is this pension reform too large or too far-reaching? – I am one of those – and I say this quite openly – who originally meant that the pension reform should continue, but I would like to admit that in itself a broad social consensus is something that corresponds to the political culture of our country. And when many Members, especially the larger government party, say in full responsibility of their mandates: We are so much worth the social consensus that we only agree if the union agrees to it, then I must respect it. An expert is light; but politics is the art of the possible, and we have proved this with this reform.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-11-07-020-XX-NRSITZ-00095_d7e18095	We shall now proceed to the debate on the request of the honourable Members. Terezija Stoisitsand Hans Helmut Moseron setting up an investigative committee on the responsibility of members of the Federal Government in connection with the free exit of the perpetrators concerning the murder of the then chairman of the DPK-I Dr. Abdul Rahman Ghassemlou and his two confidants; in particular, whether and what instructions were given in view of the threats of Iran to release "the documents on the illegal Austrian arms supplies in the first Gulf War" – as claimed by the former President of Iran Bani-Sadr. This request has now been distributed to all Members. Therefore, reading by a writer does not need to be done. The application shall have the following wording: [[Quoted print version removed]] The debate has not been called for nor has it been decided. We are therefore immediately entering the vote on the motion for a resolution. I would ask those ladies and gentlemen who wish to give their approval to this request to give an appropriate sign. – This is the minority. The application is rejected. Inflow
